ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u58	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! I present on behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights a report on: citizens' draft law on the Constitutional Tribunal, print No 550, the parliamentary draft law on the Constitutional Tribunal, print No 558, the parliamentary bill on the amendment of the Constitutional Tribunal Act, print No 569, and the parliamentary bill on the amendment of the Constitutional Tribunal Act, print No 129. The Sejm at its 20th meeting on 9 and 10 June 2016 pursuant to Article 9 of the Rules of Procedure. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm have addressed these projects to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. The committee met on 21 and 29 June. At its first meeting on 21 June, the committee appointed an emergency subcommittee for the examination of these draft laws, which I had the honour of chairing. The subcommittee held five meetings, four of which were substantive. Written opinions for draft laws were presented by the Supreme Court, the Supreme Administrative Court, the National Court of Justice, the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Treasury, the National Chamber of Legal Counsels, the office of case law and studies of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal, as well as the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights. At the beginning of the meeting on 22 June, the subcommittee agreed that the draft Constitutional Tribunal Act, print No 558, would be the basis project for further legislative work. As a decisive argument, it was pointed out that the project essentially restores the regulation of the earlier Constitutional Tribunal Act of 1 August 1997. The solutions to this law are well known to the authorities of the State, including the Constitutional Court, as it was based on the ruling in 1997 and 2015. It was noted that the project restores regulations whose constitutionality was not contested, which also did not raise major social controversy. The Extraordinary Sub-Committee concluded its work on 24 June 2016 and forwarded its report to the Committee on Justice on that date. The Commission carried out and completed its work at its meeting on 29 June. During the work of the committee and the subcommittee, the representatives of most clubs expressed their views on the purpose of adopting a new law or amending the Constitutional Tribunal Act. It has repeatedly been stressed that the new Court Act should resolve all disputes or at least a significant part of the disputes around the Constitutional Tribunal, meet constitutional standards and at the same time meet the social expectations of greater legitimacy and transparency of the Court's activities. In view of the assumption that the project would be based on the provisions of the 1997 Constitutional Court Act, during the work of the committee and the subcommittees, discussions were mainly on those solutions of the project, which constituted a derogation from the regulations in force until 2015. The proposals were discussed in detail, usually in line with the amendments tabled formally, by or inspired by the representatives of the Legislative Initiative Committee and members of the Law and Justice Clubs, the Civic Platform, the Modern Platform, the Kukiz Club15, the Polish People's Party, as well as a Member of the non-associated Janusz Sanocki. All the regulations of the attached project have been systematically discussed twice, first in the Emergency Sub-Committee and then in the Committee. The opinions submitted and during the discussion raised doubts about some of the institutions and regulations of the project. As a result, some of the doubts were eliminated or mitigated. Where this was not possible, minority proposals were submitted. After considering all four projects, the Committee on Justice and Human Rights requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law as proposed in the report of the Committee on Printing No 667. At the same time, the committee in accordance with the Rule. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 At the request of the applicants, Mr Jarosław Marciniak of the Legislative Initiative Committee of the Constitutional Tribunal Act, Ms Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz, Mr Boris Budki, Mr Robert Kropiwnicki, Mr Krzysztof Paszyk and Mr Marcin Święcicki, indicated in the printing of minority applications. I would like to thank you for the constructive cooperation you and your fellow Members, as well as all the experts and members of the committee and the subcommittee. In the event of specific questions, I shall remain at the disposal of the High House. [[Applause]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Joachim Brudziński]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u59	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Bartłomiej Wróblewski will be the first to speak. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u60	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen! [[No, sir?]] Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour to present, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, a position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. It is a good thing that the Emergency Sub-Committee and the Committee of Justice have adopted the parliamentary draft law on the Constitutional Tribunal, print No. 558, as a base project in the work on the new Constitutional Tribunal Act. It is not that it was submitted by a group of Members of the Law and Justice. This project has another decisive advantage. In fact, it crosses most of the disputes concerning the solutions adopted in the Constitutional Court Act of 25 June 2015. This is due to the fact that this proposal essentially restores the provisions of the earlier Constitutional Tribunal Act of 1 August 1997, which was in force between 1997 and 2015. In other words, the regulations which, first of all, constitutionality was not challenged by any significant political or social group and which, in any event, compared with the controversy raised by the 2015 Act have been functioning satisfactorily in practice for almost two decades. This is a fundamental advantage in comparison with the proposal to return to the original wording of the law adopted on 25 June 2015, especially if the scale of the controversy which was triggered by the adoption of that law is taken into account. As I mentioned, although the proposal essentially restores the rules of the earlier Court Act, on some issues it contains derogations from the rules in force at the time. It does not resolve all the issues that are currently under discussion, but offers an opportunity to stabilise the situation and lower the political temperature of the dispute around the Constitutional Tribunal. The adoption of this proposal ends a period of several months of legal uncertainty related to divergent opinions on the legal nature of the decisions of the Constitutional Tribunal in the period from March 2016. An additional advantage is the fact that the project provides for the publication of controversial decisions of the Constitutional Tribunal issued in the period from 10 March 2016. Some proposals respond to the criticism of the solutions of the current Court Act and correspond at least to the changes expected by the various parties to the dispute. As the citizens' draft law on the Constitutional Tribunal has been established, the contested draft also obliges or at least provides for the possibility to examine fully the constitutionality and legality of important constitutionally and socially important laws. Another of the proposals for amendments that coincide with the proposals for a civil project is that each member of the General Assembly of the Tribunal may vote for only one candidate in the selection procedure of the President and Vice-President of the Constitutional Tribunal. We also took into account the proposal of the club of the Polish People's Party, according to which judges were to be assigned to cases in alphabetical order, according to the order of influence of cases. The draft also takes into account the observations made by the Group of Courts and Studies of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Civic Platform and changes in the composition of the ruling in the second instance in disciplinary proceedings against the judges of the Tribunal. Some of the proposals contained in the draft laws and repeated in the course of the work of the subcommittee and committees could lead to the resolution of some further or other controversy concerning the Constitutional Tribunal. In particular, it was interesting to propose the Polish People's Party that judges, whose validity of the election exists, be put to rest. Unfortunately, serious and widespread reservations regarding the constitutionality of the proposal prevent this from being implemented. Similar doubts concern the solution for the judges of the Tribunal to elect the Sejm by a majority of 2/3 of the votes. To conclude by discussing our position towards the work of the committee and the subcommittee, let me stress and thank you for the participation of all parties to the political stage, although in particular for the constructive attitude of representatives of Kukiz and the Polish People's Party. In considering the opinions put forward on some of the project solutions, and in order to clarify the content of certain provisions, the Law and Justice Club is proposing an additional eight amendments. They meet the expectations of the most relentless opposition. In particular, in cases of particular complexity, we propose that only the President of the Tribunal, the formation or a group of three judges, and not, as before, may apply for full review. [[♪ The bell ♪]] was proposed, including the Attorney General and the President. We also propose deleting the provision so that, in exceptional circumstances, rulings of the Tribunal can be taken by a majority of 2/3 of the votes. [[Mr Joanna Scheuring-Wielgus: Time.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u138	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In accordance with the mandate granted to me by the Constitutional Tribunal Act to represent this institution outside, I would like to speak on the next draft act on the Court which the High Sejm is working on. My conscience - not only as president of the tribunal, but also as a citizen and professor of legal sciences - does not allow me to remain silent. Silence would be an expression of passive surrender and permission, consent to the highly thoughtless, arbitrary solutions that were found in this project. I have not been consulted before on the project's assumptions, although this is the case in every EU country with regard to the process of drafting laws governing the system and equiping the constitutional court with tools for ruling - this always happens in discussions with the presidents or the President and constitutional judges. I will refer only to the most important problems this project has caused. The question arises as to whether the Sejm can adopt any law, also openly contrary to the law, with the constitution. Sometimes you hear that Parliament is the voice of the sovereign. That's not true. [[Oh!]] Parliament shall be the voice of the representatives of the sovereign. The sovereign of the Republic of Poland is the Polish nation, not the Members and Senators. [[Applause]] [[Or the tribunal.]] Nor are constitutional ministers a sovereign; they are not judges, including judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. [[Applause]] It should also be recalled that the whole Polish nation is a sovereign, not a part of it, which voted for the political party. In a referendum, the Sovereign expressed his will, the permanent will to bind himself and his authorities with a constitution. He decided that every body of the state - including the Sejm and the Senate - was required to comply with the Constitution. Due to the binding of the parliament with the constitution and the values it protects, not every act adopted by Parliament will deserve to be called a law. A Parliamentary act consciously adopted in breach of the procedure of its formation, an act containing a deliberate non-constitutional solution, an act consciously abolishing the separation and independence of the judicial authority, an act violating the fundamental principles of the western legal culture is in fact merely an act of law. It is not the law. My vote today is to protect the Sejm and the Senate from the establishment of an apparent bill. The bill is not entitled to an act which assumes the dependence of the Constitutional Tribunal on the will of the other authorities and prevents it from acting effectively, thus actually abolishing its role as guardian of the Constitution, and defending the Constitution of each of us, constitutional judges, sworn. Since the tribunal is intended to be a non-autonomous and ineffective body, such a bill should be regarded as a manifest attempt to change the Polish state's system in the Constitution without a formal amendment of the basic law. The draft law is based on the 1997 Constitutional Court Act. However, it is not a simple repetition of it. The project contains many new elements and they will above all be unconstitutional. At the same time, it should be stressed that the Court has confirmed the constitutionality of the essential arrangements contained in the Act adopted on 25 June 2015. The repeal of this law means the resignation of a number of solutions which, in the short term, have contributed to improving the efficiency of the Court's operation, such as the simplification of the procedure for the initial control of constitutional complaints - simplification does not mean facilitating, on the contrary, the extension of the possibility of issuing judgments at classified meetings and the regulation of proceedings in cases initiated by the proposals of groups of Members or groups of senators after the end of the term of office of the Sejm and the Senate. The first provision that requires comment here is Art. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 projects where the President agrees to remove the judge from his post. The project attaches great importance to this, even though within 30 years of the court's operation there was no need to hold a constitutional judge liable for disciplinary action. The judge in such a situation, dealing with the President's authority, who will be able to annul the ruling of the disciplinary court, is sent to the judge information: ultimately, your fate depends on the President. Be the president's judge, his conclusions. [[Applause]] [[Insinuations.]] Vote in a way that will satisfy him. Whatever delicacy he would have committed, if the president's conclusions were to be heard at the request of the motion, he could be calm. No matter who's president, it's the way the judge gets addicted to executive power. The President's influence on disciplinary action is directed at judicial independence, [[Applause]] and thus in the independence and separateness of the tribunal. It is aimed at the principle of the division and balance of the authorities. It is worth noting that, for more unknown reasons, the projector, instead of focusing on detailing the procedure for selecting a candidate for the office of a constitutional judge, always choosing a top-notch lawyer, both in terms of professionalism and personality, focuses on disciplinary proceedings. In the meantime, we must remember that the constitution considers the dignity of the judge's office to be of systemic value. As we know, according to the Venice Commission, the dependence of the final decision to remove a judge from office would require a clear constitutional basis. The Sejm does not have such constitutional law, such a basis is lacking. It is also missing if the President's consent is required. The draft provides that the Court is to rule in full if the President, the Attorney General or three judges of the Tribunal so request. This solution is aimed at the constitutionally required efficiency of the procedure, in Article. 45 constitutions. It allows de facto recognition of any constitutionally insignificant matter in full composition. Worse still, in the case of three judges of any kind, or just one fifth of the court's composition, there is no time limit for filing a full case. So, regardless of the stage at which the composition of the panel on the draft decision will take place, it will be able to be transferred to full review at any time, which will necessarily result in delays in the work and will disorganize the work of the formations in other, incomplete cases. The Constitutional Tribunal is a court which does not count 100 or 150 judges, only 15. As a court in such dimensions, it is not a large court. The power of the President and the Attorney General to decide on a case in full will always be an interference of the executive authority in the jurisdiction of the tribunal. This will always violate the principle of independence of the Court and its separation from other authorities and the principle of the division and balance of the authorities. It is also unconstitutional to set a time limit for a hearing on an application, e.g. a group of Members according to the order of influence of the cases to the Tribunal. The same provision has already been declared unconstitutional by the Court in Case K 47/15. According to the Court, its independence requires it to be free to rule not only by excluding the influence of other authorities on the content of its decisions but also on the course of the procedure for issuing them. The Venetian Commission took the same position a few days later, following its ruling of 9 March, stating that the obligation to rule in a strictly defined chronological order would be incompatible with the European standard. The Court must be able to decide on certain cases before others. It is equally unconstitutional to empower the President to decide to skip the order required by the law, which obviously does not meet this recommendation of the Venice Commission. The draft law restricts the constitutional judicial powers of the tribunal to the review of the law, i.e. to what constitutes the essence of the constitutional mission of the tribunal: to uphold the hierarchical compatibility of each legislative act with the basic law. The draft disregards the Court's right to review the power to issue a normative act and disregards the right to control other elements of the normative act than the constitutional act, although, in accordance with the settled case law of the Court, accepted by all the Court's partners, also Members who have repeatedly submitted to the Court for the control of constitutionality and relied on this principle, this argument has often been presented to the Court if the law has been proceded contrary to the accepted principles of the rule of law, if the accumulation of infringements, procedural requirements contained in the laws or regulations of the Chambers has resulted in a judgment in breach of the constitutional principle of legalism. The Court has never ruled on the compatibility of such a law with the Constitution, not on the basis of laws which regulate the political position, the procedure for proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. It will be contrary to the law that four judges rule in full. Their opposition is to automatically delay the hearing in the case by at least 3 months, and in some cases by 6 months. The time limits for the adjournment of the hearing are to be rigid and do not depend on the time when the opposing Judges draw up a proposal to settle the case. In addition, if this provision were to apply in the long term, it would conflict with the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, it would create out of 15 judges those who do against each other, revenge themselves and not rule on the compatibility of the law with the Constitution. Was that the idea of conflicting with the rights of judges elected by the High House? If not, then there is an obvious mistake of the designer. It should be taken into account that the obligation to set time limits in the order of influence of the applications would also be delayed if other applications were identified than those in which the judges raised an objection. These are provisions contrary to the principle of efficient and reliable functioning of public institutions enshrined in the preamble to the Constitution. A further provision which introduces a qualified majority of the votes necessary for a non-constitutional decision should the said opposition procedure be applied by at least four Judges is also in conflict with the law. The finding of non-compliance with the Constitution or international agreement ratified with the prior consent of the Act requires a majority of 2/3 of the votes. The requirement of such a majority to give any substantive judgment by the Court is directly incompatible with the Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this Regulation. The 5th Constitution, which provides that decisions of the Tribunal shall be subject to a majority of votes. The Court has already found the provision providing for the issuing of judgments by a majority of 2/3 to be incompatible with the Article. The draft provision is unconstitutional, according to which the President of the Tribunal no longer manages the notice, but only addresses the application for the declaration of decisions of the Tribunal to the President of the Council of Ministers. The Act requires the Tribunal to settle cases brought within a year by a legal question of the court or by a constitutional complaint and not completed before the date of entry into force of the law. The draft provides for the suspension for 6 months of all pending proceedings initiated by proposals to supplement these applications in accordance with the requirements set out in the proposed law. The time limit for the suspension of proceedings shall be independent of the time when the applications are completed. Such interim regulation will inevitably result in a drastic delay in the issuing of judgments in many cases, which violates the constitutional principle of sound and efficient functioning of public institutions. The draft also contains a provision in which the decision of the Tribunal in breach of the Law of 25 June 2015 on the Constitutional Tribunal in the period from 10 March 2016 to 30 June 2016 - today we were to issue a further judgment, but because the vice-president of the Tribunal, a member of the formation of the Tribunal, is here in the Chamber, will pass a judgment by a different date - shall be published in the relevant official journal within 30 days of the date of entry into force of the proposed law. The determination of a 30-day period for the publication of judgments may mistakenly imply that its observance is sufficient to fulfil the constitutional obligation to publish the judgments of the Tribunal without delay, which is, after all, a direct result of the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this Regulation. 2 sentences of the first constitution. It's incomprehensible why art. 91 of the draft disregards the constitutional obligation to publish the judgment of 9 March 2016 in case K 47/15, which the Venice Commission considered as a starting point for resolving the constitutional crisis. I also point out that Parliament is not competent to assess the correctness of the Court's application of the provisions of the Law on the Court's ruling process. The elements of the procedure for selecting an indeterminate number of candidates for the positions of President and Vice-President of the Tribunal are unconstitutional. The draft provides that there must be at least three of them chosen from among the judges of the tribunal who, in a secret ballot, obtained the highest number of votes. At the same time, the drafter shall limit to 1 the number of votes held by the judge by a member of the General Assembly of Judges in that election act. As a result, the candidate will also be a judge who has received only one vote, e.g. That's when the other two got seven. A person who has received one vote cannot be considered a candidate for the general assembly, contrary to the will of this assembly. It's an art, isn't it? Paragraph 194. 2nd Constitution requires that candidates for President and Vice-President be presented to the General Assembly, so not against the will of the General Assembly that candidates be presented to the President. The draft indicates that the judges of the Tribunal who have taken an oath against the President of the Republic and have not taken the duties of Judge before the date of entry into force of this Act shall, as from the date of its entry into force, include and assign cases to the formations of the Tribunal. Application of Article 92 of the proposed law would lead to an unconstitutional nullification of the effects of the judgment of 3 December 2015 in case K 34/15. This would mean a clear violation of the Constitution. In its judgment in Case K 34/15, the Court removed doubts as to the constitutionality of the legal procedure for the selection of three Judges by the Sejm of the previous term of office. That is why their choice is constitutionally important, and the President is constitutionally obliged to take their vows. The President of the Tribunal has a constitutional obligation to execute the judgment in Case K 34/15 and must not allow the three persons elected to take their seats before having been effectively filled by the Sejm. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 92, however, can be understood as calling on the President of the Republic to allow three constitutional judges elected on 8 October last year to take the oath in his presence. We are talking about the Constitutional Court Act, which is one of the most important laws of the state. Nothing but an act of evil will is therefore a 14-day vacathio legis. The Court will not only not be able to adapt to the Act but will also not be able to rule on it before its entry into force if a request from eligible entities was received and the President would not exercise the power to apply for a preventive review of the Act. The Venice Commission has also indicated that the Court must be able to review the law which regulates its functioning before it enters into force. Ladies and gentlemen, it is possible and worth introducing a reform, also in order to make the Polish country stronger and more efficient. However, the boundaries of reforms are defined by a constitution which Parliament and other authorities must respect. This means that power must not be everything. I strongly stress that when you adopt the Constitutional Court Act, you cannot exceed the limits set by the Constitution. Otherwise, you will create not a bill, but an appearance of it. Western democracy is not only about the fact that parliamentary, local and presidential elections are held on a regular basis, and that power includes those who have won the most votes. It is also based on the fact that power by law is exercised by three balancing, mutually inhibiting and cooperating segments, i.e. legislative, executive and judicial powers. In 1991, John Paul II wrote in the encyclical Centesimus annus: "It is advisable that each authority be balanced by other authorities and other areas of competence that would maintain it within the appropriate limits. That is the rule of the rule of the rule of law, in which the supreme authority has the right, not the self-will of men. The basis for the three divisions of the authorities is the mutual restraint of those authorities. One of these mechanisms is the control of constitutionality, usually exercised by a constitutional court or by the Supreme Court. The mere fact that certain detailed arrangements concerning the powers of the constitutional court and the procedures in force before it are accepted in another country is irrelevant to our country. In any case, the law adopted by the Polish Parliament must correspond to our constitution. In addition, it is worth recalling that constitutional control is also carried out in other EU countries and, contrary to some statements, in the Netherlands by the Supreme Court. In 2014 I myself voted in Vienna on behalf of the Constitutional Court in favour of the request of the Supreme Court of the Kingdom of the Netherlands to become a member of the Conference of European Constitutional Courts. The State aspiring to this prestigious organization must demonstrate that its court, in this case the Supreme Court, exercises real control over the constitutionality of the law and rules incompatible with the Constitution in matters pending before the Supreme Court, refers to the general states. Since 1989, our country has consistently implemented the values of western legal culture. Its foundations are the rule of law, the primacy of the constitution and the division of power. We have done so successfully, and that is why we have naturally become members of the Council of Europe and the European Union. Today, when deciding on the adoption of the new Constitutional Court Act in the proposed form, you decide whether Poland will maintain this course or reject the values on which the western legal culture is based. Today we decide whether we will remain part of the West or whether we will begin our return to the East. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Can I have a formal one?]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u184	As I understand it correctly, the task of this law is not to solve all the problems associated with the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal. This is partly impossible - perhaps some issues would require constitutional regulation - because there are issues that cannot be regulated by any law, because, for example, I do not know, we are unable to regulate the customs that prevail in the Constitutional Court itself, whether we are able to regulate only to a certain extent. The second point is that the most fundamental objective of this law is to resolve one current one and, in my view, the most important problem, namely the emergence of two parallel legal orders in which some of the public and some of the law-abiding entities consider what the Constitutional Tribunal is doing to give decisions, to rule, and another part of the public opinion, the law-applied entities, to believe that these are merely decisions or positions of the Tribunal. And in that sense, I believe that this law is implementing this objective. She creates a legal basis that will not be questioned by anyone in her core. It seems to me a great value of this bill. The second point is that work will be carried out in the future - and here Prof. Majchrowski plays an important role - which will allow the creation of a new law on the Constitutional Court. [[Another one?]] I think it's going to be a completely different act that's going to be a target regulation. However, if we do not want these two independent views to be threatened by the work of the Constitutional Tribunal, it seems important to adopt this regulation at this point in time. [[Exactly.]] The author of these comments is three employees of the Court's office, the Group of Lawsuits and Studies. The President has made a significant reference to this document. [[Yes, sir.]] In a few months' time, a year from now, it is possible that the Constitutional Tribunal will be dealing with this law in the future. However, Mr President, wouldn't it be better because of the authority of the tribunal and because we all want to have confidence in the tribunal - and I say this not only for political reasons, but also because this is my deep conviction - to maintain more restraint? [[Yes, sir.]] Since some of these comments were made by other entities anyway, does it make sense for you to be involved at this stage in the case... [[Member Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz: This is the European standard.]] ...when soon you may have to speak on this subject in a binding manner. Such a situation must lead to a weakening of confidence in the Constitutional Tribunal, because if it were to be so that the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal is a repetition of what they have prepared in a few days, it is difficult to expect that this would not harm the authority of the institution itself. [[Yes, sir.]] I think it would mean more in this case. It is your last sentence in many cases, but why express it earlier, in such controversial forms. [[Member Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz: Because this is the European standard. The Venice Commission is writing about it.]] I would also like to refer to a few more detailed opinions of you. You were talking about disciplinary action and asking a question. whether the problem concerning the President's participation at this last stage and the President's agreement on the President's participation in disciplinary proceedings. And you were talking about the threat that the judge might become a judge of the President, because he would always be dependent on the decision that there might be such a fear that he would behave like this when disciplinary proceedings were brought, his fate would be in the hands of the President, so that the President would show mercy and not lead to this conviction, but please note that the same question can be asked whether the same dilemma could affect another situation: that the judge should not be a judge of the President of the Tribunal and that the judge would not be a judge of the majority of the tribunal. [[Exactly.]] Life experience shows, but also knowledge of the constitution, that the influence of the President on a particular judge of the tribunal is very small, because the tribunal has no instruments to influence his life situation. On the other hand, the influence of the President of the Tribunal or the majority of the Tribunal is much greater. The fear of some kind of conformism is much greater in such an environment than in relations between two completely independent bodies. I'm going to add one more point that goes beyond what you're talking about. Namely, see that, for 27 years after 1989, there was probably one disciplinary action at the Constitutional Court, and I hope that these proceedings will be rare, while the amount of emotions that this issue raises is disproportionate to the real meaning of this institution. If there were a little more trust between the parties, perhaps this issue would not raise much doubt, and perhaps no doubt, because the intention of the legislator, the intention of the projectors at this point is to protect the independence of a particular judge, and not any other purpose. And even if it were to happen that such a provision would be declared unconstitutional, this problem will not disappear, namely that it may happen that the judge of the tribunal will be under pressure from most of his fellow Members. So the creation of some mechanism guaranteeing him this independence, including with regard to the members of the tribunal, is an important goal in itself. You said, referring to the Venice Commission on the investigation of the order of impact of cases, that the Venice Commission said that cases cannot be dealt with in a strictly defined order, but these are the exceptions I have already mentioned, which are intended to ensure that cases are not dealt with in a strictly defined order. It is possible to discuss whether another mechanism should not give the tribunal, create some other safety valve, but I do not think that the recommendations of the Venice Commission prevent the introduction of a mechanism for investigating cases in order, in principle, with appropriate exceptions. You also said that, under the current provision, the Constitutional Tribunal would not have the power to examine whether the authority was competent to issue the contested normative act, because it is literally the wording which is in this law that speaks about the examination of content and mode. But I think you can understand this provision so that it also includes the possibility of examining competences. So, in short, even without correcting this provision, the tribunal will be able to carry out research to the extent that it has been for many years so far. It seems to me, too, that this is the way in which the provision for pronouncing judgments can be understood. [[The recipe is to be clear, not interpretive.]] I also have doubts about the part in which you questioned whether the 14-day vacathio legis is sometimes sufficient for the tribunal to understand the case and the other requirements which are related to it are the result of this principle. Because, of course, you can imagine that you will consider the 14-day vacathio legis to be insufficient, and then monthly, and then annually, in the case of the Constitutional Tribunal Act, but the question is whether it serves the authority of the tribunal. The Constitutional Tribunal consists of highly qualified lawyers who are familiar with the constitutional system for which it seems to me to be not a significant problem. If citizens often need to get acquainted with very complex files, and they do not have such legal knowledge, are the courts unable to know the content of the law and adapt their actions to it within 14 days? The issue which is most often highlighted in the discussions on the Constitutional Tribunal in recent months is the question of the successive laws adopted by Parliament, first in June 2015, and later in the laws adopted by the current Parliament. Let me express my personal opinion that these disputes might not have been the case if the Constitutional Tribunal had been able to retain more restraint as regards the interpretation of the Constitution's provisions if they had not read from the Constitution the content which the Constitutional Tribunal did not wish to include in it if they had avoided public appearances and political issues if they had not preceded the actions of the Constitutional Tribunal by expressing their views on the constitutionality or unconstitutionality of individual solutions. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u188	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I present to the High Sejm a draft law amending the Act on the National School of Public Administration, printing of the Polish Parliament VIII term No. 571. The amended bill amends the Act of 14 June 1991 on the National School of Public Administration in the following scope: The amended Act introduces training for employees of public administration to the statutory tasks of the National School of Public Administration. Training for public administration may instruct the school to manage the budgetary part. The training commissioned by the authorising officer of the budgetary part shall be for the staff of the office serving the authorising officer or the staff of the units under the responsibility of the authorising officer, as well as for the staff of the units supervised by the authorising officer. The implementation of central training can be commissioned by schools also Head of Civil Service, Public Service. The training school may be commissioned after an agreement has been concluded between the school head and the head of the civil service. The agreement concluded should specify the scope, conditions and modalities for the implementation of the training. The draft law also sets out the sources of compensation for the costs of the training course. For the implementation of the training provided for in the draft law, the school may receive a special grant from the resources referred to in Article. 111 The Civil Service Act, which provides that the cost of training in the civil service is to be covered by the resources allocated to the budgets of the various offices for the financing of universal training, training under the individual civil service development programme and specialist training, and may be financed from the budget reserve for the training of the corps of civil servants for the financing of central training. Training contracted to schools may also be financed by means of funds from the budgetary section at the disposal of the relevant budgetary manager. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs at its 43rd plenary meeting on 22 June 2016 accepted the draft without amendments submitted by the Government and directed it to read it in plenary. In the justification for the amendment, it is important to stress the appropriateness and validity of the amendment to the Act on the National School of Public Administration due to the implementation of training for public administration, their high level and their adaptation to the needs of public administration. The National School of Public Administration has good conditions for training, with highly qualified lecturers, modern training methods and flexible programmes adapted to the current needs of public administration. The project will allow better and faster adaptation of training to the needs of the public administration, guaranteeing that managers of administrative training have a significant impact on the programme and the nature of the training, ensuring coordination and significant impact on the scope and level of training by the Head of Civil Service. On behalf of the committee, I recommend the High House to adopt a proposed law. [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Kidawa-Błońska Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u193	Thank you very much, Member. I invite Mr Bartosz Józwiak, the Kukiz Club15.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u194	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Kukiz club15 on the draft law amending the Act on the National School of Public Administration. As indicated in the justification for the proposed amendments, this project was created by analysing the role of the National School of Public Administration in the process of training and preparation for the public service of civil servants and senior officials of the administration of the Republic of Poland, as well as the functioning of the existing training system in the civil service. The verification of the existing arrangements indicated the need to make some changes to the existing law on the introduction of an instrument giving the Head of Civil Service as well as to the budgetary components the possibility of entrusting the National School of Public Administration with training through a grant agreement. This means introducing a further entity that will be able to carry out such training and, at the same time, should allow applicants to speed up the process of carrying out these trainings, shortening the whole procedure. As indicated, the provisions of the Act currently in force define the role of the school in the process of training public administration, without at the same time providing it with all the necessary legal instruments to fulfil this function. At present, similar trainings are, in most or in full, entrusted by individual offices to independent training providers, which is of course a type of market operation, but may also cause problems with maintaining equal training standards or limit certain flows of information about the possibilities of action to improve training on similar topics for different authorities. It is also stated in the explanatory memorandum that the implementation of specific trainings which have been contracted by different authorities makes it difficult to disseminate coherent and uniform solutions in different areas independently, which is detrimental to the results of these trainings. It should also be noted that training only on the basis of the current rules often leads to actual holding back of real training activities until the training actors are identified. These are the issues of invitations to tender and other such things, which actually cause time constraints and hinder the provision of these trainings. In view of the problems described, the aim of the amendment is to introduce into the Act on the National School of Public Administration a legal instrument enabling the Head of Civil Service and the directors of parts of the budget to provide training by the National School of Public Administration. The proposed change is also justified by the long-standing experience of this unit in providing training for and high quality of public administration. In our view, the introduction of the National School of Public Administration into this pool of training providers is an element that is entirely right, because this energy and this resource of knowledge are also used by the teachers or staff of this school, for example, for what it is used for, among other things. they are intended to provide training or to improve the competence and quality of civil servants. The bill submitted assumes that the task of the National School of Public Administration is also to provide training for employees of public administration. It therefore creates the instruments already mentioned to enable the budgetary component to provide and implement training, and the Head of Civil Service also allows the organisation and commissioning of such training to a central degree. In addition, the project establishes mechanisms for financing the training entrusted to the National School of Public Administration in the form of a targeted grant or from the resources derived from the budgetary part. Like I said, it's gonna make the procedure a little bit shorter. We are not particularly concerned that this free market element will disappear. Rather, we bet that we include another valuable subject in the course of training. The Kukiz Club15 therefore supports the proposed amendments and the entire draft law amending the Act on the National School of Public Administration. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u198	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! The practice of the government's assessment shows that actual training activities, especially with regard to central training, are carried out within a limited period of time. The proposed amendment provides for the following regulations concerning the National School of Public Administration. The National School of Public Administration is to carry out training for public administration staff, the directors of the budgetary section will be able to instruct the National School of Public Administration to provide training for staff of the office serving the authorising officer or his subordinates or supervised by him. A legal basis is created to enable the Head of Civil Service to instruct the National School of Public Administration to implement central training in the civil service. The condition for the task of the National School of Public Administration of Central Training supervised by the Head of Civil Service shall be the early conclusion of an agreement between the Director of the National School of Public Administration and the Head of Civil Service in which the scope, conditions and modalities of the implementation of the training will be determined. The purpose of the proposed amendment is to establish a financing mechanism for training entrusted to the National School of Public Administration in the form of a targeted grant from the measures referred to in Article. 111 of the Civil Service Act of 21 November 2008 or of the appropriations coming from the budgetary section at the disposal of the relevant authorising officer of the budget. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u199	Thank you very much, sir. I invite Mr Ireneusz Gaina, the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u200	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on the National School of Public Administration. Education at the National School of Public Administration provides excellent knowledge and practical skills in modern administration, unique vocational traineeships abroad and in Poland, intensive language learning, as well as participation in numerous conferences and conversations concerning the functioning of the State. The listeners receive a scholarship and guaranteed social security. Graduates work at all levels of public administration in Poland and abroad. They are employed in central and field administration, in diplomatic offices of the Republic of Poland and in international organizations in various positions, including higher positions in civil service. The possibility of achieving a higher professional position is the result of the attitude, activity and competence of graduates. The explanatory memorandum of the bill states that it was created as a result of an analysis of the role of the National School of Public Administration in the process of training and preparation for the public service of civil servants and senior officials of the administration of the Republic of Poland and the functioning of the current training system in the civil service. The amendment to the Act on the National School of Public Administration aims to introduce an instrument enabling both the Head of Civil Service and the directors of the budgetary part to entrust the National School of Public Administration with training through a grant agreement. It should be noted that, with high experience and high competence of school teachers in providing training for public administration and high quality training, the National School of Public Administration should be a natural partner of public administration. However, the transfer of funds to the school in the form of a targeted grant may to some extent violate competition rules and adversely affect the labour market in the field of training companies in the small and medium-sized enterprises, whose participation in the training pool will certainly decrease. Whether this will be a minor impact, as indicated in the justification of the project, will show practice. Training services provided by a school for ministries and central offices are of the highest quality services. Given that they serve to improve the professional level of administration, they also contribute to increasing citizens' confidence in state institutions. Currently, training services for government and central administration are provided by various training entities, which does not provide a uniform, high level of training. This also hinders the flow of information on possible actions to improve training on a similar subject or nature for different offices. This results, among other things. increasing the costs of individual training and frequent retention of training activities until training providers are selected. The bill assumes, among other things, that the task of the National School of Public Administration is to provide training for employees of public administration. An instrument will be set up to allow the budgetary component managers to instruct the school to provide training for the staff of the authority serving the authorising officer or the units subject to or supervised by the authorising officer. The Head of Civil Service will be able to instruct the National School of Public Administration to implement central training in the civil service, with an agreement setting out the scope, conditions and modalities for the implementation of the training. A mechanism will be set up to finance the training provided to the school in the form of a special grant from the funds referred to in the Civil Service Act or from the appropriations from the budgetary section at the disposal of the relevant budgetary manager. The bill assumes that the changes made will result in more flexible procedures and a mechanism enabling the National School of Public Administration to fully implement its statutory tasks in the field of training of public administration officials. The Civil Service Council, which I have the honour of being a member, has given a positive opinion on the bill. Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle will support this bill and recommend that it be directed to further work in the parliamentary committees. Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The provisions in force, and in particular the Civil Service Act of 21 November 2008, require officials to continue to improve their qualifications, and the superiors, i.e. the Directors-General and Head of Offices, must ensure that subordinate workers have access to the necessary training and other forms of professional training. Training has so far been organised on the basis of generally applicable public procurement rules, but this solution has significant disadvantages. A large number of entities providing administrative training in practice make it extremely difficult and even impossible to introduce uniform standards for programmes, methodologies and, above all, the level of teaching. Sometimes, the same thematic block, implemented by different entities and for different audiences, contained different content. Therefore, participants gained diverse knowledge and skills. It is not difficult to note that such a situation may result in a reduction in the quality of services provided by the administration and may also restrict the mobility of officials within that administration. That is why it is advisable, I repeat, because it is not sufficient and 100%, for obvious reasons, which I am about to say, to harmonise or centralise the system of organising training for officials. First of all, we want to create legal conditions for entrusting them to one specialised entity to ensure that their high level and uniform programming standard are maintained. There is no doubt that such an entity is the National School of Public Administration, due to, for example, more than twenty years of experience in conducting training in the field of law, administration, public finances, management, public policies, practical applications of information technology in official work and foreign languages. Each year, the offer of the national school includes more than 100 training courses - both in the open and closed form. These trainings are of very high quality, e.g. In 2013, over 94% of their participants reported in anonymous surveys that thanks to these trainings they significantly increased their professional qualifications. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The conclusion of this agreement, which was already raised in the statements of my previous speakers, will not be preceded by a lengthy tendering procedure, which will, first of all, improve, secondly, speed up the process of preparing and conducting the necessary training. The solution proposed by us is well-known and widely used in the countries of the European Union and certainly does not affect the competitiveness of the training market under any circumstances. Once again, I'm going to quote some hard data. No economic operator is favoured because the national school is not an economic operator. This is, I will remind you, an individual of the public finance sector and cannot be treated the same as commercial market players. Our proposal does not prohibit the selection of trainers for administrative staff through procedures provided for by the Public Procurement Law. I'm about to show that this market, when it comes to budget resources, is still, you can say, powerful. This solution will only provide a substantive and economic alternative to these procedures, but in terms of percentages, a small but important alternative. The decision to choose how to organise the training is left in the hands of the managers of the budgetary parts, i.e. the heads of the offices, the organisational units under which the employees are to be trained. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u216	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Home Affairs and Administration, I present the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Law on Civil Status Acts, prints No. 545 and 649. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! In the second half of 2014, in the previous term, the Law on Civil Status Act was proceded in the Polish Sejm. The Act entered into force on 1 March 2015. After a year of operation of the Act, and especially since 1 September 2015, when the transitional period ended, during which the work of civil status offices was supported by applications supporting the system of state registers, it turned out that a quick amendment of the Act was needed. It turned out, High Izbo, that just from September last year both local governments and civil status offices, as well as citizens, including the Ombudsman, pointed to the inconveniences associated with the functioning of the law. The main point was that the waiting time for documents issued by civil status offices was considerably longer, especially in large cities. There have been cases of waiting up to six weeks for a copy of the death certificate. The system of state registers, including the Source application, therefore required improvements, because, as I said earlier, especially in large cities due to remoteness, i.e. the possibility of applying anywhere in the country for a copy of civil status, the period of waiting for documents has increased considerably. In response to these expectations and problems, the government prepared an appropriate bill, and the Board of Administration and Home Affairs on 22 June 2016 at first reading responded positively, and this, High Izbo, unanimously, to the draft law amending the Law on Civil Status Acts. Now, in brief, I will present to the High House the most important new detailed solutions. Thus, first of all, provision will be made for the possibility for civil status officials to be authorised by the head of that office to transfer civil status records to the civil status register. I would like to add that there are over 360 000 such operations per month on the national scale. in all offices, it's a huge number. Until now, these activities could only be performed by the steering wheel or their deputies. Now these activities will also be able to be performed by authorised employees. This will speed up the transfer process. Secondly, it will be possible to continue to operate applications supporting the system, including the possibility of issuing copies from these applications. Thirdly, the possibility of administrative annulment of civil status acts by extending the powers of the voivode and the heads of the civil status office in this respect is introduced by appropriate provisions. Fourthly, at the request of the Polish Episcopate Conference, procedures related to the so-called will be improved. Concordat vows. Fifthly, a 5-year period will be introduced to supplement the civil status register with data from those acts which were created during the transitional period from March to September last year. This will make it possible to issue copies of civil status records according to the new model using data collected in support applications during this period. In addition to the above, the draft law clarifies the provisions concerning judicial experts and interpreters, as well as the number of copies of ex officio copies. The provisions concerning the submission of applications to the consul by persons residing outside the country are also clarified. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! All these changes will definitely speed up the work of civil status offices throughout Poland, and in particular, as I said, in large cities. The project was welcomed in the consultation. The bill is supported and expected by all the environments I mentioned earlier. The Committee on Internal Affairs and Administration requests the adoption by the High Chamber of a draft law amending the Law on Civil Status Acts annexed in print No 649. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u220	Thank you, Marshal. High Seym! Mrs. Marshal! On behalf of the Civic Platform club, I have the honour to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the government's draft law amending the Law on Civil Status Acts. In connection with the functioning of the system of state registers, civil status offices have requested a correction, which aims to achieve the objective indicated at the outset. The proposed amendments include the granting to employees of civil status offices the powers to transfer to the civil status register the acts drawn up in the civil status books. The ability of employees to transfer to the register is one of the most desirable changes and most frequently requested. This will allow for a significant improvement of the work and thus speed up the handling of patents. Another important change is the introduction of an administrative attempt to annul civil status records and additional notes wrongly drawn up. As the applicant shows in the submitted project, in many cities several dozen acts are expected for annulment, most are in Wrocław, where more than 110 acts are indicated. It therefore seems appropriate to make it available as soon as possible. The next important amendment is to allow the issuing of copies of civil status certificates drawn up in civil status books for a period of 5 years from the date of entry into force of the proposed act using data collected in applications supporting registration, kept in offices prior to the implementation of the central register. This amendment aims to avoid possible upsizing of tasks in offices and to minimise difficulties in the work of offices, with a five-year period for supplementing the civil status register. In order not to extend any longer, since the entire law has been very carefully referred to, I would add that the project does not contain technical regulations, the project is also not subject to the need to agree with European Union law. The Civic Platform supports the solutions presented in view of this project, recognising the validity of its implementation. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u224	Dear Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! I was honored to present the position of the Poselski Club in relation to the government's draft law amending the Law on Civil Status Acts. As part of the public administration reform, the System of State Registers was introduced and a central IT application Source was implemented at civil status offices on 31 March 2015. This was intended to improve the work of civil status offices and facilitate the service of citizens by introducing inter alia. so-called. location, i.e. possibility to apply for copies of civil status certificates at the office regardless of place of residence. However, the IT application The source has not functioned properly since the beginning of its operation, in conjunction with additional administrative responsibilities, linked to localisation and excessive formalism - the need for every civil status worker's confirmation by the head of the office or its deputy - resulted in a partial paralysis and a significant extension of the deadline for dealing with citizens' affairs, particularly in large voivodeship cities. It should therefore be noted that the proposed statutory amendments meet the expectations of civil status offices. The fundamental change in the possibility for the head of the civil status office of an office to transfer to the civil status register the acts drawn up in the civil status books will certainly speed up the process of migration of acts into the central system and, above all, will allow for more efficient handling of citizens' affairs. There is no doubt that amendments to the new provision allowing civil status registers to be supplied with civil status files drawn up in support applications from March to August to be introduced by 2015, however, due to problems with the application, the Source has not been introduced. It also deserves support to amend the provisions allowing for a period of 5 years from the date of entry into force of the proposed law to make copies of civil status records based on data contained in applications supporting the registration of civil status prior to the implementation of the Central System. This should help to remove the backlog of citizens' cases in the largest cities in the country, but it will certainly not solve the issue of correct rules for the functioning of the system of state registers. This is needed for this moment a prosthetic which, I hope, will not turn into a permanent solution. The process related to the migration of data from supporting applications and the migration of civil status records from the civil status books to the central register is extremely complex. If it is properly implemented by the Ministry of Digitalization during this five-year period referred to in the Act, it should be considered as a success, which I first wish the Poles and Poles, because it will certainly improve the handling of many matters in civil status offices. Changes to the bill are also introducing solutions to correct misregistration of civil status records and additional notes, which were created for technical reasons or as a result of incorrect application of the functionality of the central application by users. The above-mentioned amendments, as well as a number of provisions allowing for the elimination of differences in interpretation concerning already existing regulations, or which complement the legal gaps, allow this draft law to be considered necessary and worthy of support. Finally, I would like to express my hope that the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, and in particular the Department of Civil Affairs, will also listen to the proposals for technical changes proposed by the Association of Civil Officers, which, without legal interference, can certainly solve a number of specific problems affecting civil status officials, and the consequence may be a significant improvement in the process of issuing civil status documents. The Modern Posel Club will support the proposed amendments to the Law on Civil Status Act. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u228	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The initial period of operation of the System of State Registers revealed the shortcomings of the Act of November 2014, which the current amendment is intended to bring to an end. One of the major changes introduced by this law is undoubtedly the granting of powers to employees of the civil status office to transfer to the civil status register acts drawn up in the civil status books. To date, the need for the head of office or his deputy to authenticate each time the acts are transferred extends the period of transfer to the system of state registers, the time of waiting for the citizens for the requested copy of the act and the purposeless burden on the managers and their alternates. Delegating this obligation to the employees appointed by the head of staff - because of his knowledge of the competences and skills of his subordinates - can only have a positive impact on the quality and speed of office work. The improvement of the work of the Offices is also of the nature of the other amendments introduced by the procedated Act. The five-year period in which civil status certificates may be issued, in which no amendments need to be made without prior transfer to the Central Register, is certainly one of them. This will undoubtedly contribute to the elimination of the current arrears at civil status offices, especially in large urban agglomerations. I also consider it a step in the right direction to extend the powers of administrative annulment of civil status acts and additional references. Both the extension of the powers of voivodships in the event of the annulment of acts relating to the same event and the powers of the heads of civil status offices in the event of the annulment of acts misassembled or misregistered, I consider it reasonable. Both will have a simple impact on speeding up the withdrawal of these acts from legal trading and on the smooth functioning of civil status offices. The designer's regulations take into account the demands, among others. civil status offices and represent the expected reasonable and significant changes, including technical changes, which contribute to improving and improving the quality of the functioning of these offices and the service of their citizens. The Free and Solidarni Circle will support the bill. Thank you very much.

Now we have a deadline of 1 October, and this is a deadline which is indicated by the Central Information Technology Centre, a unit subordinate to the Minister for Digitalization, as this is the minimum time necessary for technical changes to function in the register. As far as the other changes are concerned - yes, we do want to make it possible, in fact, to facilitate the annulment of all those acts which have been introduced with error, have been introduced twice - here the voivode - or have been introduced due to technical error, the error of the system. Well, I will use this example in maritime education. Well, if a person owns a Volkswagen car, which is produced in Germany, it's rather difficult to imagine that this car would go to Germany for a review. On the other hand, the car owner of this brand bought it in Germany. About this is the difference between the repair yard and the production yard. Renovation shipyards, which in Poland are yards serving the de facto closed Baltic market, with a growing number of ships and still closed in the sense that it is a market, in addition to the Kaliningrad circuit, de facto intra-EU, and in each of the countries that have such repair yards, there is a CIT tax, PIT, VAT, is allowed and illegal public aid, there are similar solutions. Therefore, interference with renovation will immediately end with the European Commission's interference and, in fact, be regarded as unfair competition for such solutions. Paragraph 112. The Council for Nuclear Safety and Radiological Protection shall act as an advisory and advisory body with the President of the State Agency for Atomic Energy. So far, the Act on Nuclear Law only regulates the functioning and composition of the Council. On the other hand, there was a lack of rules governing the cessation of membership of the Council and provisions allowing the members of the Council to be dismissed or resigned, even in the case of health problems preventing the member from taking part in the work of the Council. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u299	Position of the PSL Parliamentary Club vis-à-vis the bill amending the Act on Nuclear Law. The direct objective of the amendment to the Act is to give new powers to the Minister of the Environment to appoint and dismiss vice-presidents of the State Agency for Atomics and the Council for Nuclear Safety and Radiological Protection. Currently, Vice-Presidents of the State Agency for Atomistics are appointed and dismissed by the Minister of the Environment at the request of the President of the Agency from among the persons selected by the competition. After the change, the Minister will be able to dismiss the Vice-Presidents in the absence of seat of the President of the Agency. The Act also amends in the case of the Council for Nuclear Safety and Radiological Protection, which acts in front of the President of the State Agency for Atomistics as an advisory and advisory body. To date, members of this board have been appointed by the president of the agency. In accordance with the proposal, the Minister for the Environment is to appoint them after consulting the President of the Agency. The applicants point out that the legal arrangements so far indicated that a person appointed to the Council for a four-year term could practically not give up sitting on the Council on his own. There was also no mechanism to establish a replacement, e.g. For the sake of death. The club agrees with the need for clarification, but we believe that the President of the Council for Nuclear Safety and Radiological Protection (BJOR) should have competence in this area. The PSL Club has a negative assessment of the bill.
