ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u303	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of Law and Justice, I would like to comment on the Act amending the Health Protection Act against the consequences of tobacco and tobacco products. There are a number of reasons why the draft law amending the Health Protection Act was adopted against the consequences of tobacco and tobacco products. Amendments to the current rules are necessary. On 20 May 2016, the European Union Tobacco Products Directive entered into force, the provisions of which relate inter alia. to buy and sell e-cigarettes and nicotine fluids for re-filling them. The revised law implements the provisions of the Directive. We hope that these regulations will discourage potential smokers from reaching for an e-cigarette advertised as healthy. Potential smokers are young people whose health we have a particular responsibility to care for. Smokers and passive smokers are exposed to substances that cause the development of civilizational diseases and to bad effects that lead to premature death. 1,800 thousand. is the estimated number of e-burners in Poland. The cost of treatment related to smoking-related diseases amounts to about PLN 18 million per year, while losses in the economy due to absenteeism or reduced productivity at work are estimated at about PLN 15 billion per year. The numbers speak for themselves - smoking is nothing but loss, no profit. The Act provides for a ban on the sale of e-cigarettes to minors, a ban on smoking in public places, a ban on placing information in the store: a ban on the sale of tobacco products, electronic cigarettes or back-up containers to persons under the age of 18. A ban will also be imposed on producers and importers of advertising and sponsorship of sports, cultural, educational and health events. Retail trade in e-cigarettes and nicotine liquids on the Internet will also be banned. According to the draft law, the sale ban will also be covered by taste cigarettes and self-rotation tobacco, excluding those with the addition of menthol, which will apply from 2020. Furthermore, the use of substances suggesting that the tobacco product is healthy will be prohibited. The Act introduces an obligation to include graphic and text health warnings on individual packages and summary warnings. There will be a warning on the package: Smoking kills, throw now, and the information: The tobacco smoke contains more than 70 substances causing cancer. The e-cigarette packages will contain information about all the ingredients they contain, their quantities and health warnings. The Act provides for inspection of the packaging of tobacco products by means of a special unique identifier and other tamper-resistant security features. There is also a requirement to obtain a marketing authorisation for novel tobacco products. The aim of the amendment is to protect the health of Poles, protect passive smokers and correct economic losses. The law and justice recommend the above-mentioned law in a positive way. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u304	Thank you, Member. I invite Mrs Alicia Chybicka, the Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u305	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Position of the Civic Platform Club on the draft law amending the Health Protection Act against the consequences of the use of tobacco and tobacco products. The draft law implements EU directives on the prohibition of advertising in particular of e-cigarettes and the prohibition of smoking in public places. Population of developmental age more than 7 million human beings must be protected from all forms of smoking, active and passive, conventional cigarettes and e-cigarettes, as well as modern cigarettes. For the population of developmental age, all forms of contact with active and passive smoking of all kinds of tobacco products are dangerous: cigarillos, cigars, innovative tobacco products such as electronic cigarettes. The toxicity, i.e. the extent to which a substance can cause harmful effects on the body, is significantly higher for the child than for the adult. The addiction, or condition that affects the child's ability to control his behavior, is faster in the child. Children and adolescents must not be informed that vitamins and other additives, caffeine, taurine, colouring additives reduce the harmfulness of cigarettes. The advertising of tobacco products and their derivatives should not be introduced in any form, because the health of the whole society and, above all, of children is the most important. For the last 25 years, we have achieved a great deal, but we are still at the beginning of the road. It still burns as many as 8 million Poles. Because of smoking, tens of thousands of adult Poles die every year, half of them young and middle-aged. So also in the next decade the most important public health challenge in Poland will be measures to reduce the number of smokers. There are realistic grounds for halving smoking rates from around 8 million in 2015 to 4 million in 2025-2030. This will lead to halving of premature tobacco mortality for young and middle-aged adults. It will also be an important element increasing human capital and will improve the demographic structure of Poland. It should be recalled that, for the next 10 years too, ordinary cigarettes will remain the biggest challenge for public health in Poland. Ordinary cigarettes still smoke eight times more people than e-cigarettes. Ordinary cigarettes are far away, several rows of size, more dangerous to health than other nicotine dispensing devices, and this applies of course to biological effects. I'm not talking about the problem of children and the social consequences of e-cigarettes. E-cigarettes are a relatively new product on the European Union market and evidence of their potential risks and benefits is only beginning to emerge. On the other hand, it would be ridiculous to say that an e-cigarette, a nicotine dispenser, is healthy. I've never heard such a thesis. Is nicotine also toxic in small doses? Nicotine is also a highly addictive substance that meets all the requirements of the drug definition. We also know from scientific research that nicotine even at low doses is cardiotoxic. E-cigarettes are considered as a means of helping to quit smoking and so-called. a measure to reduce the health damage to smoking, i.e. Harm reduction. It should be recalled, however, that e-cigarettes are far less effective as a means of helping to quit smoking than the centrally active medicines on the market. The best example of such a drug is the widely used cytisin in Poland, using which almost half a million smokers try to break their addiction every year. Medicines such as cytidine most effectively help quit smoking, do not lead to addiction like e-cigarettes, their production is subject to all pharmaceutical safety rigors and are only available in pharmacies. However, the real problem with e-cigarettes is their use by children who, when smoking e-cigarettes, learn to inhale poisonous and addictive substances. Inhaling nicotine steam with nicotine dispensers is becoming increasingly an opportunity for children to gloat, and the consequence is the contamination of the body of young people with poison. The use of e-cigarettes becomes a school or a vestibule for children to use other addictive substances. Therefore, the availability and sale of this instrument should be regulated in Poland. In the future, new generations will also become a special problem. new nicotine products. This is the direction of the European Union's new tobacco directive, which, I understand, has been strongly supported by the Minister of Health Konstanty Radziwiłł for some time, earlier with a similar bill was presented by the former Minister of Health Bartosz Arłukowicz, and very close to its adoption was the Minister of Health Marian Zembala. We are entering a new phase of the fight against smoking, where the most important thing is to provide help to those who are determined to quit smoking, [[♪ The bell ♪]] or medically speaking, treating patients with tobacco addiction. Some studies show that 80% smokers in Poland declare their willingness to quit smoking. Considering the above. arguments, Parliamentary Club The Civic Platform supports the bill, and I appeal to all my fellow Members from all options to set an example and not smoke, and to promote non-smoking. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applause]]

The regulation of electronic cigarettes should take place considerably earlier, irrespective of international obligations. However, the assessment of the solutions proposed today is very difficult. Changes in sharpening, bans and requirements are particularly true for people who use nicotine. We see a high level of dissatisfaction among people, organisations and institutions involved in promoting e-cigarettes. The discussion around the proposed changes reminded us of the difference between the harmfulness of electronic and traditional cigarettes. However, it is the responsibility of parliamentarians to adapt the rules in a clear way. We treat electronic cigarettes as harmful to health, which, in the light of objective studies, especially due to the presence of addictive nicotine, is beyond doubt. One of the most important problems of recent years is the marketing effect in relation to so-called. e-cigarettes, creating an illusion of no harm. The clarification of terminology has made it clear that smoking of both types of cigarettes has been identified. As a result, the amendment of the Act solves key problems: banning the sale of e-cigarettes or spare containers - with nicotine-containing liquid - to persons under 18 years of age, reducing their smoking sites, banning the sale of cross-border tobacco products, introducing restrictions on advertising and promotion of these products, introducing new health warnings on packaging. These are urgent and important issues. We're doing it today. The regulations that raised our doubts are primarily about equalizing smokers of both types of cigarettes. In practice, people using electronic cigarettes, e.g. with the need to reduce health damage by avoiding inhaling thousands of harmful substances contained in a traditional cigarette, they will be exposed to passive smoking and inhalation of this smoke in common smokers of traditional and electronic cigarettes designated for smoking. I believe that, in the absence of results from objective studies, clearly determining the degree of harmfulness derived from e-cigarettes, those using this form of nicotine should use other smoking sites than traditional cigarettes. At the same time, in view of emphasising the addictive nature of nicotine, it is impossible to leave this form of smoking without restrictions on the place of smoking. The rigorous nature of the law, which absolutely emphasizes the responsibility of users of nicotine for possible or current addiction, together with the consequences of smoking difficulties, results from the treatment of nicotine as a psychoactive substance, which is justified by the classification proposed by the World Health Organisation. It is therefore worthwhile to support those who do not benefit from nicotine, while emphasising the value of health, and to enable them to live without cigarette smoke in any form. The correctness and correctness of this law will be verified by the degree of compliance and enforcement. In the area of tobacco products we see today so-called. a gray zone, of a huge scale, which primarily concerns trade in tobacco without excise duty. I hope that today the project under consideration will not deepen the problem of non-compliance with the existing law. The preparation, coordination of offices and institutions, as well as the commitment and conviction of the correctness of the changes made, can be a guarantee of its effectiveness. [[♪ The bell ♪]] On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u353	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Location of buildings and buildings, trees, shrubs and the execution of earth works in the vicinity of railway lines. In the beginning, I would like to inform you that the Civic Platform Club supports amendments to the Rail Transport Act, because they will speed up investment and we have a lot of work ahead of us that needs to be done, which is why this special act is necessary. However, I regret that this project is a parliamentary project, not a government project, and therefore there is a greater likelihood of errors. As we read in the explanatory memorandum of the bill, the main purpose of the regulation is to introduce simplification solutions into the system and speed up the work related to the preparation and implementation of rail infrastructure investments. This is certainly the right assumption, but it is not possible to lose sight of the elements relevant to persons and institutions to which the new law will affect. My concern is art. 57a. The provision that the derogation must not endanger the safety of rail traffic makes it a dead record. Rather, it should be noted that the derogation will not result in an unacceptable increase in risk, since a tree that has been hit by the tracks always creates such a danger. There are also mouths in this article. 2, which raises doubts. In this paragraph, it shall be stated that the derogations shall be granted by the governor. This is not the correct record, because if it is the ground up to a width of 15 m and the railway area, i.e. the closed area, then you need to know that the oldosta does not work in closed areas. This is the jurisdiction of the voivodship. So there may be a competence gap when the governor says that this is not his territory, and the voivode finds that the law imposes a derogation on the old lady and there will be no one to decide, and that is not what this law was about. That is why we propose this change. This is a very important change to assign these competences immediately. The prohibition also raises the question of Article. 9o, which states that in the event of the disposal of the ownership or right of use of perpetual immovable property covered by the application for a decision to determine the location of railway lines after the notification of the initiation of the procedure, the purchaser and the seller shall be obliged to notify the relevant voivode of the data of the new owner or perpetual user within 7 days. I think it's a little exaggerated, too, because 14 days would be the optimal time, the right conditions. Another question is the obligation imposed on the railway infrastructure manager to adapt safety management systems to the provisions of the Act within 3 months of its entry into force. This provision indirectly involves a period of vacathio legis, 14 days. These are too short terms to introduce and test changes. As we know, in neighbouring countries, this testing is more than a year old. These are safety issues, and this is very important for passenger safety and infrastructure in general. Therefore, we should extend the terms: vacathio legis - minimum 30 days, adaptation of safety management systems - at least 6 months. The last element that raises doubts is the proposal that local authorities should, however, be able to waive the right to compensation for the land used for the construction of railway lines. This may be part of the pressure on the authorities of local government units requested by the investor to decide, and it must be remembered that the plots in different parts of Poland have different values. For some people, the donation of the plot will not be a problem, for other local authorities it may be a significant loss to the budget. [[♪ The bell ♪]] It is certainly good to have it in the nearest surroundings of the train station and railway station, but it is not the best solution to put local governments before deciding to give up compensation. In conclusion, most of the provisions introduced will certainly speed up and facilitate the development of rail networks, but some provisions of the project need to be further improved. The Civic Platform, as I said initially, supports the entire bill, but the two amendments I have been talking about will be acceptable. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u393	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the Act amending the Law on Government Departments and some other laws contained in print No 642, the report of the committee in print No 657. It is a simple, seemingly draft law transferring competence from one ministry to another in its main records. And we would probably have accepted this project unreservedly, but again something happened that shouldn't have happened and what really became the norm in this House. Namely, among the provisions governing the transfer of powers, there is a provision in the article. 57, which unfortunately has negative consequences for our local governments. In general terms, the Minister responsible for regional development, in agreement with the Minister responsible for construction, planning and spatial planning and housing, can define, by means of a regulation, the criteria and indicators for areas of regeneration enabling the financing of municipal regeneration programmes from the budget of the European Union or the state budget - including the provision of unlimited possibilities for interference with municipal regeneration programmes. Our doubts relate to the lack of precise writing to which the criteria and indicators described are to refer. As we know, areas of regeneration must comply with the statutory criteria. At the same time, the Act gives municipalities the opportunity to establish specific problem areas, adapted to local circumstances, and municipalities were obliged to use objective and verifiable test methods. The indicators to be carried out by the revitalisation project are also indicated in each operational programme from which the project will be financed. The definition of additional criteria and indicators for areas of regeneration at the level of the Minister's Regulation poses a risk of manual control in the identification of areas of regeneration and design of actions undertaken by municipalities in this area. Such a Regulation as referred to in Article. 57, will define a rigid framework - because such will be conditioned by indicators - and in this rigid framework will have to include regeneration programmes. This will absolutely centralise decisions on key issues for the development of local communities. The process of social participation and its impact on the programming and implementation of revitalization projects will be completely affected. There isn't, and we won't be able to do that. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform makes two amendments. One is the deletion in the article. 57, point 1. The second amendment concerns Article. 27 Amendments 7. This amendment aims to preserve the Article in the Law on spatial planning and development. 47a, which reads: "The Minister responsible for regional development shall make proposals for changes to the Council of Ministers, taking into account, in particular, the concept of spatial development of the country, as regards the development of spatial governance, the review and analysis of the principles, the way in which the institutions, procedures and instruments operate. The resignation of the Minister in the field of regional development from the function of a monitoring body for the state of spatial governance and recommending a solution for its improvement will be detrimental to the development of the country, as spatial governance is the main measure of sustainable development. I hereby submit these amendments and ask the High House, and before that, the committee, to accept them. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

These are, of course, legitimate changes. The draft also provides for the repeal of the Article. 23a point 2a of the Law on Government Administrations, which is the result of the amendments introduced by the Law of 19 November 2015, which provided that all matters relating to spatial planning and management, without further differentiation to local, national and regional levels, would fall within the competence of the Minister responsible for construction, spatial planning and development and housing. Consequently, we will also find provisions that fall under the heading: Regional development, programming and implementation of regional policy now include urban policy and the coordination of programmes and actions for the regeneration of socially and economically degraded areas, and the project therefore repeals point 2b of Article. 23a. The above solutions are also reflected in the Article. 27 draft amendment to the Law of 27 March 2003 on spatial planning and development, where the project introduces inter alia. a change receiving a statutory authorisation as set out in Article. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u429	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a statement on behalf of the Poselski Club Modern about the draft law amending the Act on the promotion of the sustainable development of the fishing sector with the participation of the European Maritime and Fisheries Fund. The Modern Posel Club fully supports the changes proposed in the project. This draft de facto merely clarifies and amends the wrong wording and legislative errors of the Act of 10 July 2015. It was written for the previous government. From the very beginning, both the team that formed it and the parliamentarians of the previous term were fully aware of the numerous substantive errors that are currently being repaired by the parliamentary project. The Act was written by the Department of Fisheries in the Ministry of Agriculture, and the current amendments were already made in the same department, only in the newly created Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping. In both cases, both the Act and the current amendments were dealt with by a team of persons chaired by the same persons transferred in the framework of the reorganisation. These amendments should have been introduced a long time ago. The question arises as to why this happened, that neither the previous government nor the present government was in a hurry to bring them into the bill. The absence of these amendments has led to a serious delay in the implementation of the operational programme for the years 2014 and 2020 and has already been for up to 9 months. This delay is so incomprehensible that it is known that the aid resulting from this aid scheme in the Union Regulation expires at the end of 2017. Even assuming that the legislative changes in law can be implemented quite quickly, it is necessary for this law to come into force in practice, after all, that it needs an implementing regulation and a procedure, which will take months to come, and knowing the Polish reality, even years. The most affected are fishermen who cannot benefit from the aid scheme. Unfortunately, there are a lot of chances that they will not benefit at all, because Poland will not be able to use these funds by the deadline. There is therefore a serious risk that e.g. Shipowners will not be able to physically scrap their ships. The absence of this aid with simultaneous reductions in fishing quotas and decreasing catches can contribute in general to the wave of bankruptcy. In summary, rather than helping ship owners, the government will contribute to their serious problems. In view of the above, I would like to stress that the state-of-the-art is in favour of completing the legislative work on this project as soon as possible and implementing it so that the shipowners in the fisheries sector will be able to benefit from the assistance they have dedicated to them and that Poland will not be forced to return all this assistance back to the European Commission. Thank you very much.

The proposed amendment to the Act contains regulations the matter of which falls within the competence of the Member State specified in the abovementioned Regulations. The proposed provisions shall specify, inter alia, the content of the statutory delegation on the basis of which the fisheries minister shall identify the relevant authorities or bodies providing legal or economic advice on the promotion of sustainable, resource-efficient, innovative, competitive and knowledge-based aquaculture. In order to deliberately spend funds, it is necessary to clarify the question of the list of beneficiaries who may benefit from the envisaged forms of support. Part of the changes to the provisions of the Act are of a sequencing nature. This regulation will not have any financial implications for the state budget and is compatible with European Union law and the project itself has positive social, economic and legal effects. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u439	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I have the honour to present to the High House a report on the draft law on retail tax. The key objective of the regulation is to align or, for example, approximate the conditions of operation on the economic market of large commercial networks and small family shops. During the work on the Law, it turned out that these actions were extremely difficult, but in the end, the committee has developed solutions that allow the objective to be achieved. Existing disparities on the commercial market so far, among others. in taxes, they have caused large trading networks to grow at a galloping pace in recent years, and small family shops have disappeared from the market. The report was presented to you in print No 691. The legal solutions adopted in the draft introduce an additional tax in the tax system in force. The revenue generated from this tax in part will be directed by the government towards the implementation of the current one from 1 April this year. 500+ program, program supporting Polish families in raising children. The remaining part of the funding will be allocated to the state budget. The provisions adopted have been widely consulted and consulted. The Parliamentary Team to Support Entrepreneurship and Economic Patriotism is a major part of the consultation process of adopted solutions. Every business community interested in this project was heard and invited by the team to work together to develop optimal solutions. In the contested draft law, the government proposed that the new income tax should be charged to retailers whose revenues exceeded the statutory revenue in a month. The proposed project proposes a tax-free amount for retailers, while the surplus obtained above is proposed to be subject to retail tax. The amount of the tax-free amount was estimated by the government at PLN 17 million per month. This amount will be calculated, this revenue will be calculated on a compound basis each month. This gives a tax-free amount of PLN 204 million per year. The solutions adopted have been developed and selected from a number of proposed and analysed solutions. In the first stage of the work, a lower tax-free amount was considered. Even the retail community itself estimated this amount at a lower level than the government finally proposed in this project. The discussion on the tax model also took into account the introduction of a linear tax rate, the analysis of whether to accept several levels of tax progression. The possibility of introducing a higher tax on sales revenue on Saturdays and Sundays and the possibility of adopting a lower tax-free amount with an excess of the linear tax rate was considered. Finally, the retail tax model we have developed today with full responsibility can be seen as the most optimal, while the tax procedure adopted is simple and transparent. Tax progression in this solution is mild, two-step. In the first threshold, which includes the value of revenue obtained above the amount of tax-free, i.e. PLN 17 million but not exceeding PLN 170 million, the taxpayer charges tax at a rate of 0.8% tax bases, while revenues above PLN 170 million will be taxed at 1.4%. Six amendments were received by the committee during the work on the bill, including. applicants proposed a 50% reduction in tax rates This Decision shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. Three of the six amendments tabled were accepted by the Commission, including an amendment extending the deadline for the implementation of the Law of 1 August 2016 to 1 September 2016. At the original date of application of the Law from 1 August 2016, the government estimated that by the end of 2016 a gross amount of about PLN 630 million would be paid to the state budget, while net revenue was about PLN 510 million. In view of the delay in the implementation of this law by 1 month, it is expected that these revenues will be lower this year. The government estimates that next year, in 2017, in which the law will be in force throughout the year, the revenue from this will amount to PLN 1.9 billion in the budget and this is gross respect. The committee bureau also received a request for a public hearing. The Commission voted against this proposal. I would like to thank Members working on the Act in the Committee on Public Finance, and I would like to thank Members working on the resolution of the Act in the Parliamentary Group for the Support of Entrepreneurship and Economic Patriotism. On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I ask the High House to adopt the Retail Tax Act. It is a good bill and expected on the market. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The tax base will be the surplus of retail revenues over PLN 17 million in a given month and will relate to revenue generated from now until the end of the month, which means that income to be achieved by the taxpayer up to a total amount of PLN 17 million will not be taxed. Consequently, the tax-free amount will amount to PLN 17 million per month, PLN 204 million per year. This tax will help to increase the revenue of the state budget, will directly fund the implementation of the State aid scheme for raising children From this point of view, the social impact needs to be assessed positively. The applicant also assumes that the introduction of the tax will not result in an increase in prices and that a large competition between trading networks and possible strengthening of competition will not allow the cost of the tribute to be passed on to the consumer. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u481	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The project entered the Sejm on May 6. Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the draft law to the Committee on Energy and the Treasury for first reading on 23 June 2016. The Commission carried out its first reading and examined the draft at its meeting on 5 July. The draft amendment to the Commercialisation and Privatisation Act aims to optimise the use of the funds collected in the reprivatisation account. The draft replaces the solution adopted in Article. 69f Act, which allowed the grant to be granted to m.st. Warsaw, extending the range of potential beneficiaries of payments to all local governments. The proposed amendments to the Act grant the Treasury Minister the opportunity to invest in the funds of the reprivatisation fund. The Minister responsible for Treasury Affairs also obtains in 2016 the possibility of increasing the share capital of the Polish Television, Polish Radio and regional radio companies in the amount not exceeding PLN 30 million. During the process of reviewing the bill and the substantive discussion, Members proposed a number of amendments, which the committee had given a negative opinion. These amendments, as requested by the applicants, were submitted as minority requests. The Commission has given a positive opinion on two self-corrections. One self-correction concerned the addition to the proposed Article. Paragraph 56 1 point (1) (a) c the formulation of a financial contribution to the satisfaction of the former owners of property acquired by the State Treasury, in particular by the issue of the goods. This amendment clarifies the provisions of the Act and extends the number of cases where support for local government grants will be possible. In turn, in the proposed article. 69h is changing automatically from PLN 20 million to PLN 30 million. The Commission asks the High House to adopt the proposed amendments and adopt the draft. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u484	Thank you very much. Mr Zdzisław Gawlik will now speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u485	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club comments on the Parliamentary bill amending the Commercialisation and Privatization Act. In principle, the proposed novel concerns one issue, namely the Reprivatisation Fund. The Reprivatisation Fund is a special-purpose fund which is intended to collect funds intended to cover claims of persons who have been affected by nationalisation processes in the past, i.e. they have been deprived of rights in favour of the State, i.e. claims of persons who expect reprivatisation. The manner in which the fund is created, the management of the funds collected for the fund shall be determined by the Commercialisation and Privatisation Act. This fund - it should be noted - is created from privatisation revenues. As long as Poland does not implement the process of reprivatization, it is necessary to maintain the Reprivatisation Fund with care that the amount of funds collected in this fund should be as high as possible. Recent years have shown that the passage of time and awareness of Poles make spending from this fund more and more from year to year. It is not only about keeping funds for current payments, to cover the current needs of former owners deprived of their rights by the legislation of the People's Poland. As much money as possible is needed to cover current needs resulting from judicial judgments or administrative decisions, but funds to satisfy any owner of the victim since 1944. Only then can this idea of justice be realized. Therefore, it is negative to assess any solution that is aimed at reducing or merely creating a risk of reducing the funds raised for the Reprivatisation Fund, and this law, this novel, creates at least two such dangers. The first is the allocation of up to PLN 30 million to the capital increase in 2016. Polish Television, Polish Radio and regional radio companies. The second risk is related to the law, with the provision of the Minister of the Treasury with the right to purchase from these funds collected on the stock fund public companies. There is no rational justification why the funds raised in the special fund would be spent on the financing of radio and television. I can agree that such a need may sometimes exist, but the State Treasury Minister has at his disposal a number of funds which he can use and which can be used to finance radio and television. They are funds dedicated to this purpose. Ladies and gentlemen, similar attention should also be given to the intention to involve funds from the fund in the purchase of shares of public companies. Nor is such a proposal justified. If such a need to continue the nationalisation processes existed, the Minister of the Treasury has the resources, including the Fund for the Restructuring of Entrepreneurs, to make such spending of funds for the purchase of shares. Projectors also request to extend the range of beneficiaries, entities which may apply for funds collected for the Reprivatisation Fund, other than Warsaw, local self-government units, as well as to remove this deadline limiting the right to apply for these funds by the end of 2016. However, it is necessary to see different legal regimes in which Warsaw and other local government units are located. The process of nationalisation was the acquisition of property by the State, by the State Treasury, not by a local government unit. Therefore, whenever the State Treasury Minister decides to finance these reprivatisation processes, he must take into account the sequence of events and consequences that have taken place and have led to the fact that a specific local government unit has become the owner of the law, which is then denied. The adoption of this solution - to equip or extend the beneficiary's circle to other local government units that can apply for these measures - gives rise to risks and concerns, whether in the future, when such a time comes, because it will always come to satisfy former owners, someone will not want to reach the local government units to cover those measures which will, not: to collect those funds which are necessary to satisfy these claims, also those local government units participated. We also point to this risk that when the chosen ones use these funds, everyone else is not obliged to pay for the reprivatisation. For these reasons, the Parliamentary Club of Law... [[Voice from the courtroom: Law and Justice, bravo!]] ... The Civic Platform makes four amendments to the proposed act [[♪ The bell ♪]] Legally, so that these changes, which also imply the right of local government to benefit from these measures, are not merely a verbal declaration. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The main assumptions of the present law are that local governments from all over the country, and not so far Warsaw, will be able to receive grants from the Reprivatisation Fund for the payment of claims against the Treasury made by former owners of assets taken over by the State Treasury. Previously, the law provided for the possibility of grants only for the city of Warsaw. It's positive. The grant will continue to be optional, the decision will be taken by the Treasury Minister. The local government will have to demonstrate the specific circumstances justifying the grant. This may continue to discriminate against certain local authorities, but it is still a good direction. The funds of the fund will still be available in 2016 to increase the share capital of the Polish Television S.A., Polish Radio and regional radio companies. And here we are dealing with the use of the Reprivatisation Fund's assets for purposes that are not necessarily intended. So the money that was supposed to be used, among other things. to create a reprivatization bill, will be used to sustain companies of the Treasury taken over by party clans. The Treasury Minister is given the opportunity to invest funds of the Fund in shares of public companies. Same charge as above. A minimum level of funds of twice the expenditure incurred in the previous financial year is required. The bill comes on the day of the announcement. The Reprivatisation Fund is a State-owned special-purpose fund established under Article. Paragraph 56 1 point 1 of the Commercialisation and Privatisation Act of 30 August 1996. Funds from sales of 5% are collected on the fund account shares belonging to the State Treasury in each of the companies arising from commercialisation and interest on those funds for the purpose of satisfying claims by former owners of property taken over by the State Treasury. At the end of 2015, the fund was close to PLN 5 billion, while expenditure from the fund over the last 5 years was on average about PLN 290 million per year. What conclusion? ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u494	Thank you very much. Mr Ireneusz will now take the floor to gain from the Free and Solidarnia wheels.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u495	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Commercialisation and Privatization. The draft law submitted is a corrective regulation which removes the previously adopted article. 69f of the Act a solution providing for the possibility to grant grants to m.st. Warsaw. The legislative initiative aimed at providing financial assistance to all local government units for their implementation of re-privatization claims by heirs, former owners of nationalised properties, was long awaited by local authorities. In the Reprivatisation Fund account, funds are collected from sales of 5% shares belonging to the State Treasury in each of the companies arising from commercialisation and interest on those funds for the purpose of satisfying claims by former owners of property taken over by the State Treasury. The Act grants the Treasury Minister the opportunity to invest the accumulated funds. The accumulated funds may be used to increase the portfolio of shares held by the Treasury. The revenue generated by the shares purchased will be the revenue of the fund, which will allow for greater flexibility in the planning of payments in the long term. Under the Act, the Minister of State Treasury will be able to increase the share capital of Polish Television SA, Polish Radio SA and regional radio companies in the amount not exceeding, in accordance with the amendment adopted at today's meeting of the Commission for Energy and Treasury, a total amount of PLN 30 million. This competence involves the emergence of a special situation for public media this year. Finally, the Act provides for temporary maintenance under the Decree of the Minister of State Treasury of 29 June 2010 on the detailed manner of conducting the financial management of the Reprivatisation Fund, but not for more than 3 months from the date of entry into force of the Act. The need for this solution is dictated by a short deadline for the entry into force of the proposed law, i.e. on the day following the date of publication and the need to adapt the content of the Regulation to the amendments proposed in the draft law. However, it should be noted that there are also some doubts about the shape of the regulation. The projectors proposed that the funds collected in the Reprivatisation Fund account should be used to implement the agreements by paying the benefits resulting from final judgments, court settlements and final administrative decisions issued in connection with the nationalisation of property. It should be noted that the projectors assumed that the grant constituted aid if the JST met its re-privatisation claims only through the payment of benefits and that therefore the funds derived from the grant would not be able to support local authorities in the event of decisions requiring local authorities to repay their real estate in kind. This is a very serious limitation, because it is the obligation to return the property in nature that is the primary problem of local government units. In addition, the subsidy referred to in Article. Paragraph 56 1 point (1) (a) c, in accordance with the proposed Article. Paragraph 56 4e of the Commercialisation and Privatisation Act may be granted if the financial resources collected in the fund account so permit. This is a non-precision provision, in particular in the absence of a regulation on the management of the Reprivatisation Fund and in the absence of clear findings as to the conditions to be met by the local government unit. Finally, on the basis of the article. Paragraph 56 4e the amended act must specify the specific circumstances justifying the grant, including those relating to its financial situation preventing the self-fulfilment of the claims in question. The specific circumstances justifying the grant are not indicated. The above-mentioned issues require as much detail as possible. It is up to them whether or not an applicant for a targeted grant will receive support. Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle will support the bill submitted, while pointing to the need to clarify the above mentioned details in order to remove possible interpretation doubts. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u566	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the applicants, I have the honour to present a draft law amending the Energy Law Act and some other laws. The aim of the proposed changes is to improve the energy security of Poland and to organise the fuel market. The aim of the project is also to address the shortcomings and shortcomings of the existing provisions of the Energy Law, which give rise to interpretation doubts or create difficulties in the application of the law. The adoption of the solutions contained in this project will allow for greater transparency in fuel and natural gas markets and will improve the competitiveness and sealing of the concession system and thus reduce the grey zone in liquid fuels. The phenomenon of the grey zone hits the economic development and energy security of Poland, disrupts the proper functioning of the market and hinders equal competition. According to the available data, the scale of the grey zone in Poland in the period 2012 the total consumption of diesel. Our country lost at least PLN 3 and 4 billion for extortion in this area. On a smaller scale, this phenomenon occurs in the area of motor gasoline and LPG. The problem is the unregulated and unregulated supply of fuels mainly from the Kaliningrad region, from the territory of Lithuania, Belarus and Germany. The main areas of the grey fuel market are: extortion of VAT using vanishing entities, sale of fuel oil for propulsion purposes, sale of lubricating oils for propulsion purposes, smuggling of fuel oil. In the case of LPG, the Polish LPG Organization estimates that in the autogas sector alone the phenomenon is so-called. the gray zone is 5.7% total turnover and annual sales of autogas are around 1.9 million tonnes. However, it is difficult to obtain accurate and reliable data here, because some are imported from the east. The main areas of LPG offences concern the use of the difference in LPG taxation for traction and heating purposes, the sale of fuels not meeting the quality requirements and the reporting of LPG re-exports to countries that do not consume significant quantities of LPG. The most important changes proposed in this law and will reduce these phenomena include: sealing the concession system and ensuring real control by the President of the URE, introducing a uniform definition of liquid fuels, sealing the process of the first introduction of liquid fuels into the domestic market, sealing the system of maintaining mandatory natural gas stocks, which will protect the Polish state from circumvention of the tax obligation and protect Polish consumers from interruptions of gas supply. Unfortunately, the current regulation makes it possible to circumvent this obligation, which is why the proposal envisages extending it, while providing for flexibility in the implementation of the gas stock obligation. Other changes in the project, some. The project removes legal barriers to the supply of liquefied gas to the gas terminal in Świnoujście, introduces the supervision of the Minister of Energy of the planned construction or demolition of transmission pipelines and equips the Polish state with tools to effectively resolve conflicts between the owner of the network and the operator on this system. The financial consequences of the Act, approx. PLN 4.5 million, concern posts at the Energy Regulatory Office for carrying out the tasks of the Act, and revenues to the state budget are estimated at PLN few hundred million to PLN few billion. Practice will clearly show the consequences of this bill. In conclusion, the Act will restore mechanisms for fair competition in liquid fuel markets, including natural gas, to increase revenue to the state budget. The Act will also provide greater protection for consumers against unfair market players, make the market more transparent and allow economic operators to verify their counterparties. The law will also eliminate, or at least very much reduce the grey zone and restore the principles of fair competition, increase the energy security of Poland. Therefore, I recommend to the High House that the bill be adopted, and before that I encourage you to work on the substantive work on the draft in the Committee on Energy and the Treasury. I would add that the project presented is compatible with EU law. The law on which we will work is consistent is a complement to the solutions contained in the draft law on the amendment of the act on the tax on goods and services in the Senate, since the Sejm adopted this document. And so much justification from the applicants. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u578	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Members' Circle of Free and Solidarni, I present my position on the proposal for a parliamentary bill amending the Energy Law Act and some other laws. The aim of the applicants was to implement legislative changes implementing a number of systemic changes in the regulation of the fuel and natural gas market, which concern the fuel market institutions. Their common denominator was to be to organise and introduce greater transparency in the markets. The proposed changes include: system-ordering of the liquid fuel market; increasing State control over the oil and fuel intervention stock system; ordering the natural gas market; extending the obligation to maintain mandatory natural gas stocks and introducing a new legal form for the implementation of this obligation through the so-called "market." a ticket agreement; the elimination of shortcomings and shortcomings in the legislation in force in the field of liquid fuels and natural gas, which raise interpretation doubts or create difficulties in the application of the law. Pathological phenomena in the liquid fuel market are dynamic. Fuel trafficking offences are taking new forms, and the continuing administrative regulation of this market even encourages operators to engage in illegal activities. The aim of the projectors was to create an administrative framework for market entry and functioning in order to maximise the execution of all public-law obligations. The purpose of amending the Act, which was to system-order the liquid fuel market, to increase State control over the system of intervention stocks of oil and fuels, to organise the natural gas market, to extend the obligation to maintain mandatory stocks of natural gas and to introduce a new legal form for the implementation of this obligation, so-called. a ticket agreement, the elimination of shortcomings and shortcomings in the legislation in force in the field of liquid fuels and natural gas, which give rise to interpretation doubts, or cause difficulties in the application of the law - it seems that has not been achieved. The excess regulation contained in the bill does not make the fuel market more transparent and will not help economic operators verify their counterparties. The removal of the shadow economy and the restoration of the principles of fair business is done through the implementation of advanced technologies to prosecute such crimes, not the creation of a statutory umbrella for various legal combinations. The proposed law provides for a number of detailed changes to existing legal institutions with difficult to predict effects. Excessive regulation limits the ability of the executive authorities of the State to act. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1b (1) of the Energy Law is incompatible with Article. 49 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. After World War II, the Jewish population and Polish intelligence were seriously destroyed and destroyed by the Nazis and lost their city rights, becoming a settlement. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u610	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On June 28, 1956, in Poznań, a mass performance of workers took place. The cause of the protests was a general dissatisfaction with the situation in the country and region. The direct reason for the strikes, and then the street protests, was the conflict at Hippolite Cegielski's Plant at that time called, otherwise, the Zakłady im. Stalin. Workers demanded better working conditions and safety, better wages. After a fiasco of talks, the protesters went out into the streets, where a crowd of 10,000 gathered. The slogans were chanted: 'We want to live normal', 'We want bread for our children', 'Before with the red bourgeoisie', 'Before with the communists', 'We want Catholic Poland, not the Bolshevik Poland'. The demonstrations turned into riots and street fights. The military, UB and Militia brutally pacified the protest. More than 10,000 were concentrated in Poznań. Soldiers, 359 tanks, 31 armored guns, over 900 cars and motorcycles. The balance of events was tragic. At least 57 people were killed, more than 600 injured. During these events the communist prime minister Cyrankiewicz said: "Every provocator or madman who dares to raise his hand against the people's power, let him be sure that the people's power will cut off his hand." Despite the blockage of information, news of these events spread throughout the country, expressing their support for protesting workers and solidarity with those who faced communist power. Poznań events, more and more often referred to as the Poznań Uprising, occupied by the blood of so many victims did not go to waste, accelerated the process of democratisation in the country, became a milestone towards free Poland. It is difficult to imagine today's Republic without the heritage of Poznań June. In free Poland, every year the celebrations of these dramatic events took place in the atmosphere of dignity, peace and reflection. Unfortunately, this year's anniversary ceremony was used by opponents of the present government as a arena of rudeness, contempt for national values and contempt for heroes of June.56 It appeared through whistles, humming, jamming speeches by so-called. Democrats. This was done by the refusal of the local authorities of Poznań to play the national anthem by the military orchestra. Personally, I cannot understand the intention and find justifications for the President-in-Office of Poznań, Jacek Jaśkowiak, whose sick political procedures led to the desecration of the ceremony taking place with participation, among others. President of Poland Andrzej Duda and President of Hungary Janos Ader. I would like to express my support for Zbigniew Hoffman's statement by the voivode of Wielkopolska, who, when judging these regrettable incidents, said that there was a desecration of the holiday, but above all, to humiliate the heroes of June and their families. He also deeply regretted the insurgents of June and their families, as well as all those who had been affected by the events. The Academic Civic Club also issued a statement on this matter. President Lech Kaczyński in Poznań, stating that it was the President of Poznań who decided to use the anniversary celebrations of Poznan June, 56 for the current political game, to promote the CODE. The statement stated, inter alia, that:: The President of the Republic was treated as an intruder. Not by the Poznań people. By people suffering from hatred for what has for centuries been forming the Polish national identity and the Polish love of freedom, by people who cannot accept the democratic will of the nation and that they have been deprived of the privileges of their own authority. Solidarity with this statement AKO in Poznań was expressed by Academic Civic Clubs. President Lech Kaczyński in Cracow, Gdańsk, Katowice, Lublin, Łódź, Toruń and Warsaw. As a citizen of the proud, free Republic, Member of the Polish Parliament and at the same time member of the Academic Civic Club im. President Lech Kaczyński in Lublin pays tribute to the heroes of June.56 At the same time, I regret and regret that, as the president of the city of Poznań, a place sanctified by the blood of heroes, one can present such an anti-Polish, anti-Polish and shameful attitude. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-05-sejm-22-1.u616	A statement by the Members on the 70th anniversary of Zdzisław Badocha's death. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On June 28 we celebrated the 70th anniversary of the death of Zdzisław Badocha ps. Iron - soldier of the Home Army and commander of Squadron 2. The Vilnius AK Brigade in the post-war years. I'd like to say a few words about the life of this enchanted soldier. Zdzisław Badocha was born on 22 March 1925 in Dąbrowa Górnicza. His father was a P.O. of the Border Protection Corps. At the age of a dozen, Baden and his family lived in New Saints, where he attended their junior high school. Józef Piłsudski. At the age of 13, he became a member of the Scout Team. B. Głowacki. He began his conspiracy in the Baden National Army at the age of 17. He became a member then. 23. Ignalino Diversion Center - New Saints. A year later, he took command of one of the patrols. In 1944, after his previous deconspiracy, he became a soldier of Sergeant Mieczysław Kitkiewicz's platoon. Kitka in 5. The Vilnius AK Brigade under the command of Zygmunt Szendzielarz a.k.a. Scoundrel. Badocha took part in the fight for the liberation of Vilnius as part of Operation Ostra Gate. When in July 1944 the 5th brigade was surrounded by the Soviets, its members scattered. Badocha and other soldiers were then incorporated into the 4th spare infantry regiment in the Dojlids. In October of the same year, the Irony joined Major Zygmunt Szendzielarz's 5th Vilnius brigade. As commander of the platoon in the 4th squadron of Lt. Pluciński's ps. Mścisław, who remained at the age of 20, continued to fight. 22 May 1945 Badocha became a corporal. A few months later, he was promoted to lieutenant. When the 5th Brigades were demobilized in September of the same year, Badocha visited his family in Dąbrowa Górnicza. He then left to his relatives a collection of photographs of soldiers of the 5th brigade with dedications. The 5th brigade resumed its operations at the turn of February and March 1946. Badocha became commander of the 2nd squadron, and he and his men carried out a whole range of activities related to disarming military patrols and precincts. One of the actions thanks to the BBC radio station became known to the public abroad. Here we are talking about events in Stara Kishev, where employees of the local Public Security Office were shot. In response to the actions of the 2nd squadron, the communist authorities sent large forces against him. Badocha was attacked on June 10, 1946. Two officers of the Security Office and three militiamen were killed as a result of the fighting. The squadron managed to escape, but the Iron One himself was wounded. He was taken to the Czernin estate for treatment, which was run by a Kashubian activist for several months working with the 5th brigade. Badocha, however, was released. Information about his whereabouts has been released to the authorities previously arrested liaison Regina Żylinska. On 28 June 1946, a dozen operating groups surrounded the estate. Iron tried to free himself from the lap. But he was shot with a grenade shrapnel. Badocha's burial site is unknown. It wasn't until the 1960s that his family found out about the circumstances of his death. Zdzisław Badocha for outstanding merits incurred in the fight against the weapon in his hand for the sovereignty and independence of the Polish state in 1939 and 1956 was posthumously decorated with the Cross of the Cross of Independence in 2015. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you.
